## Comprehensive Codebase Exploration Report: repiq

### **1. Project Purpose & Vision**

**repiq** is a fast, objective metrics CLI for AI agents selecting OSS repositories and libraries. Built for use by Claude Code, Cursor, Copilot, and similar agents that need structured, non-reasoning data retrieval.

**Core Philosophy:**
- No judgments, recommendations, or scores — just raw data
- Agent-first design with deterministic JSON/NDJSON output
- Role separation: `repiq -> data collection` / `AI -> reasoning` / `human -> final decision`
- Speed-optimized for vibe coding workflows (3-second target)
- Minimal viable scope initially, expanding across ecosystems

---

### **2. Current Features & Providers**

**Supported Providers:**

| Provider   | Scheme Format          | Metrics Available | Status       |
|-----------|----------------------|------------------|--------------|
| GitHub    | `github:<owner>/<repo>` | 8 metrics (stars, forks, open issues, contributors, releases, activity, 30d stats) | Implemented |
| npm       | `npm:<package>` or `npm:@scope/package` | 5 metrics (weekly downloads, version, publish date, dependencies, license) | Implemented |
| PyPI      | `pypi:<package>` | 7 metrics (weekly/monthly downloads, version, publish date, dependencies, license, Python requirement) | Implemented |
| crates.io | `crates:<crate>` | 7 metrics (total/recent downloads, version, publish date, dependencies, license, reverse dependencies) | Implemented |
| Go Modules | `go:<module>` | 4 metrics (version, publish date, dependencies, license via deps.dev) | Implemented |

**Output Formats:**
- `--json` (default) — Single JSON array
- `--ndjson` — Newline-delimited JSON (one per line)
- `--markdown` — Separate Markdown tables per scheme

**Key Features:**
- Parallel fetching of multiple targets (any mix of schemes)
- Disk caching with 24-hour TTL (SHA-256 hashed keys)
- GitHub authentication (gh CLI token → GITHUB_TOKEN env → unauthenticated fallback)
- Graceful error handling with partial failures (available metrics returned even if some APIs fail)
- CLI built with Go standard library (flags only)

---

### **3. Architecture & Patterns**

**Core Components:**

```
internal/
├── cli/                  # Entry point, arg parsing, orchestration
├── provider/             # Provider interface, metrics structs, registry
│   ├── github/          # GitHub API implementation (google/go-github)
│   ├── npm/             # npm registry API
│   ├── pypi/            # PyPI + pypistats.org APIs
│   ├── crates/          # crates.io API
│   ├── golang/          # Go proxy + deps.dev APIs
│   ├── provider.go      # Provider interface, Result & Metrics structs
│   ├── registry.go      # Simple provider lookup (scheme -> provider)
│   └── target.go        # Target parsing (scheme:identifier)
├── cache/               # Decorator pattern caching
│   ├── provider.go      # CachingProvider wrapper
│   └── store.go         # File-based store (SHA-256 keys, atomic writes)
├── format/              # Output formatters (JSON, NDJSON, Markdown)
├── auth/                # GitHub token resolution
└── cmd/repiq/main.go    # CLI entry
```

**Key Design Patterns:**

1. **Provider Interface Pattern:**
   ```go
   type Provider interface {
       Scheme() string
       Fetch(ctx context.Context, identifier string) (Result, error)
   }
   ```
   - Scheme-based registration in a simple Registry
   - All metrics in unified Result struct with omitempty fields
   - Errors in Result.Error field (not Go error return) for CLI graceful degradation

2. **Decorator Pattern (Caching):**
   - `cache.NewProvider()` wraps any Provider
   - Applies TTL-based disk cache transparently
   - Supports `--no-cache` flag (reads fresh but writes to cache)

3. **Parallel Fetching:**
   - sync.WaitGroup + sync.Mutex pattern within each provider
   - Multiple API calls per provider (e.g., GitHub contributors, releases in parallel)
   - Multiple targets fetched concurrently at CLI level

4. **Error Handling:**
   - Provider validation errors → Result.Error (no Go error)
   - API call failures → partial Result with available metrics + error message
   - All-fail scenario → empty Result with error only

---

### **4. Tech Stack**

- **Language:** Go 1.24.13
- **External Dependencies:**
  - `github.com/google/go-github/v68` — GitHub API client
  - Only dependency (all other providers use stdlib net/http)
  
- **Build System:** Nix flake (`flake.nix`)
- **Testing:** Go's built-in `testing` package + `httptest` for mock servers
- **Linting:** golangci-lint
- **CLI:** stdlib `flag` package (no frameworks)

---

### **5. Current CLI Capabilities**

```bash
# Single repo (JSON by default)
repiq github:facebook/react

# Multiple repos with mixed schemes
repiq github:facebook/react npm:react pypi:flask crates:tokio go:golang.org/x/net

# Markdown table (schema-grouped)
repiq --markdown github:golang/go

# NDJSON (one per line)
repiq --ndjson github:facebook/react github:vuejs/core

# No-cache (always fetch fresh)
repiq --no-cache github:facebook/react
```

---

**Key Lessons Documented:**
- LRU eviction terminology
- Bug: deps.dev uses `:requirements` endpoint (not `:dependencies`) for Go packages
- Rule: Always verify external API endpoints with curl before implementation

### **8. Code Statistics**

- **Total Go code:** ~2,723 lines (all providers, CLI, cache, format)
- **Latest commit:** `80f302a` — feat(provider): add PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules providers
- **Spec documents:** 22 markdown files across 5 epics
- **Test coverage:** Unit tests + httptest mock servers for each provider

---

### **9. Provider Implementation Pattern Reference**

Each provider follows a consistent pattern (documented in `docs/adding-a-provider.md`):

1. **Define Metrics Struct** in `provider.go`
2. **Implement Provider** in `internal/provider/<scheme>/<scheme>.go`:
   - `Scheme()` method
   - `Fetch()` method with identifier validation
   - Parallel API calls (sync.WaitGroup + sync.Mutex)
   - Error collection for partial failures
3. **Write Tests** with httptest mock servers
4. **Register in CLI** (`internal/cli/cli.go`)
5. **Add Markdown formatter** (`internal/format/format.go`)

**Common Headers Used:**
- `User-Agent: repiq (https://github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq)` (crates.io requires this)
- GitHub: OAuth token via custom http.RoundTripper
- All: 30-second timeout on http.Client

---

### **10. Key Files to Know**

| File | Purpose | Size | Key Insight |
|------|---------|------|-------------|
| `internal/provider/provider.go` | Provider interface & metrics definitions | 72 lines | All metrics in one Result struct |
| `internal/cli/cli.go` | Orchestration, flag parsing, parallel fetching | 168 lines | Provider registration & caching |
| `internal/cache/store.go` | File-based cache with SHA-256 hashing | 90 lines | Atomic writes, TTL-based expiry |
| `docs/adding-a-provider.md` | Step-by-step provider addition guide | 292 lines | Best practices checklist |
| `docs/specs/overview.md` | Project vision & problem statement | 28 lines | "Agent-first, data only" mantra |

  const [shuffled, setShuffled] = useState(logos);
  useEffect(() => {
    const shuffle = items => {
      const copy = [...items];
      for (let i = copy.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
        const j = Math.floor(Math.random() * (i + 1));
        [copy[i], copy[j]] = [copy[j], copy[i]];
      }
      return copy;
    };
    setShuffled(shuffle(logos));
  }, []);
  const row1 = shuffled.filter((_, i) => i % 2 === 0);
  const row2 = shuffled.filter((_, i) => i % 2 === 1);
  const row1Doubled = [...row1, ...row1];
  const row2Doubled = [...row2, ...row2];
  return <>
      <div className="logo-carousel">
        <div className="logo-carousel-track" style={{
    animation: 'logo-scroll 50s linear infinite'
  }}>
          {row1Doubled.map((logo, i) => <a key={`${logo.name}-${i}`} href={logo.url} style={{
    textDecoration: 'none',
    border: 'none'
  }}>
              <img className="block dark:hidden object-contain" style={{
    width: logo.width || '150px',
    maxWidth: '100%'
  }} src={logo.lightSrc} alt={logo.name} />
              <img className="hidden dark:block object-contain" style={{
    width: logo.width || '150px',
    maxWidth: '100%'
  }} src={logo.darkSrc} alt={logo.name} />
            </a>)}
        </div>
      </div>
      <div className="logo-carousel">
        <div className="logo-carousel-track" style={{
    animation: 'logo-scroll 60s linear infinite reverse'
  }}>
          {row2Doubled.map((logo, i) => <a key={`${logo.name}-${i}`} href={logo.url} style={{
    textDecoration: 'none',
    border: 'none'
  }}>
              <img className="block dark:hidden object-contain" style={{
    width: logo.width || '150px',
    maxWidth: '100%'
  }} src={logo.lightSrc} alt={logo.name} />
              <img className="hidden dark:block object-contain" style={{
    width: logo.width || '150px',
    maxWidth: '100%'
  }} src={logo.darkSrc} alt={logo.name} />
            </a>)}
        </div>
      </div>
    </>;
};

Agent Skills are folders of instructions, scripts, and resources that agents can discover and use to do things more accurately and efficiently.

## Why Agent Skills?

Agents are increasingly capable, but often don't have the context they need to do real work reliably. Skills solve this by giving agents access to procedural knowledge and company-, team-, and user-specific context they can load on demand. Agents with access to a set of skills can extend their capabilities based on the task they're working on.

**For skill authors**: Build capabilities once and deploy them across multiple agent products.

**For compatible agents**: Support for skills lets end users give agents new capabilities out of the box.

**For teams and enterprises**: Capture organizational knowledge in portable, version-controlled packages.

## What can Agent Skills enable?

* **Domain expertise**: Package specialized knowledge into reusable instructions, from legal review processes to data analysis pipelines.
* **New capabilities**: Give agents new capabilities (e.g. creating presentations, building MCP servers, analyzing datasets).
* **Repeatable workflows**: Turn multi-step tasks into consistent and auditable workflows.
* **Interoperability**: Reuse the same skill across different skills-compatible agent products.

## Adoption

Agent Skills are supported by leading AI development tools.

<LogoCarousel />

## Open development

The Agent Skills format was originally developed by [Anthropic](https://www.anthropic.com/), released as an open standard, and has been adopted by a growing number of agent products. The standard is open to contributions from the broader ecosystem.

[View on GitHub](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills)

# What are skills?

> Agent Skills are a lightweight, open format for extending AI agent capabilities with specialized knowledge and workflows.

At its core, a skill is a folder containing a `SKILL.md` file. This file includes metadata (`name` and `description`, at minimum) and instructions that tell an agent how to perform a specific task. Skills can also bundle scripts, templates, and reference materials.

```directory  theme={null}
my-skill/
├── SKILL.md          # Required: instructions + metadata
├── scripts/          # Optional: executable code
├── references/       # Optional: documentation
└── assets/           # Optional: templates, resources
```

## How skills work

Skills use **progressive disclosure** to manage context efficiently:

1. **Discovery**: At startup, agents load only the name and description of each available skill, just enough to know when it might be relevant.

2. **Activation**: When a task matches a skill's description, the agent reads the full `SKILL.md` instructions into context.

3. **Execution**: The agent follows the instructions, optionally loading referenced files or executing bundled code as needed.

This approach keeps agents fast while giving them access to more context on demand.

## The SKILL.md file

Every skill starts with a `SKILL.md` file containing YAML frontmatter and Markdown instructions:

```mdx  theme={null}
---
name: pdf-processing
description: Extract text and tables from PDF files, fill forms, merge documents.
---

# PDF Processing

## When to use this skill
Use this skill when the user needs to work with PDF files...

## How to extract text
1. Use pdfplumber for text extraction...

## How to fill forms
...
```

The following frontmatter is required at the top of `SKILL.md`:

* `name`: A short identifier
* `description`: When to use this skill

The Markdown body contains the actual instructions and has no specific restrictions on structure or content.

This simple format has some key advantages:

* **Self-documenting**: A skill author or user can read a `SKILL.md` and understand what it does, making skills easy to audit and improve.

* **Extensible**: Skills can range in complexity from just text instructions to executable code, assets, and templates.

* **Portable**: Skills are just files, so they're easy to edit, version, and share.

## Next steps

* [View the specification](/specification) to understand the full format.
* [Add skills support to your agent](/integrate-skills) to build a compatible client.
* [See example skills](https://github.com/anthropics/skills) on GitHub.
* [Read authoring best practices](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/agent-skills/best-practices) for writing effective skills.
* [Use the reference library](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills/tree/main/skills-ref) to validate skills and generate prompt XML.

# Specification

> The complete format specification for Agent Skills.

This document defines the Agent Skills format.

## Directory structure

A skill is a directory containing at minimum a `SKILL.md` file:

```
skill-name/
└── SKILL.md          # Required
```

<Tip>
  You can optionally include [additional directories](#optional-directories) such as `scripts/`, `references/`, and `assets/` to support your skill.
</Tip>

## SKILL.md format

The `SKILL.md` file must contain YAML frontmatter followed by Markdown content.

### Frontmatter (required)

```yaml  theme={null}
---
name: skill-name
description: A description of what this skill does and when to use it.
---
```

With optional fields:

```yaml  theme={null}
---
name: pdf-processing
description: Extract text and tables from PDF files, fill forms, merge documents.
license: Apache-2.0
metadata:
  author: example-org
  version: "1.0"
---
```

| Field           | Required | Constraints                                                                                                       |
| --------------- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `name`          | Yes      | Max 64 characters. Lowercase letters, numbers, and hyphens only. Must not start or end with a hyphen.             |
| `description`   | Yes      | Max 1024 characters. Non-empty. Describes what the skill does and when to use it.                                 |
| `license`       | No       | License name or reference to a bundled license file.                                                              |
| `compatibility` | No       | Max 500 characters. Indicates environment requirements (intended product, system packages, network access, etc.). |
| `metadata`      | No       | Arbitrary key-value mapping for additional metadata.                                                              |
| `allowed-tools` | No       | Space-delimited list of pre-approved tools the skill may use. (Experimental)                                      |

#### `name` field

The required `name` field:

* Must be 1-64 characters
* May only contain unicode lowercase alphanumeric characters and hyphens (`a-z` and `-`)
* Must not start or end with `-`
* Must not contain consecutive hyphens (`--`)
* Must match the parent directory name

Valid examples:

```yaml  theme={null}
name: pdf-processing
```

```yaml  theme={null}
name: data-analysis
```

```yaml  theme={null}
name: code-review
```

Invalid examples:

```yaml  theme={null}
name: PDF-Processing  # uppercase not allowed
```

```yaml  theme={null}
name: -pdf  # cannot start with hyphen
```

```yaml  theme={null}
name: pdf--processing  # consecutive hyphens not allowed
```

#### `description` field

The required `description` field:

* Must be 1-1024 characters
* Should describe both what the skill does and when to use it
* Should include specific keywords that help agents identify relevant tasks

Good example:

```yaml  theme={null}
description: Extracts text and tables from PDF files, fills PDF forms, and merges multiple PDFs. Use when working with PDF documents or when the user mentions PDFs, forms, or document extraction.
```

Poor example:

```yaml  theme={null}
description: Helps with PDFs.
```

#### `license` field

The optional `license` field:

* Specifies the license applied to the skill
* We recommend keeping it short (either the name of a license or the name of a bundled license file)

Example:

```yaml  theme={null}
license: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms
```

#### `compatibility` field

The optional `compatibility` field:

* Must be 1-500 characters if provided
* Should only be included if your skill has specific environment requirements
* Can indicate intended product, required system packages, network access needs, etc.

Examples:

```yaml  theme={null}
compatibility: Designed for Claude Code (or similar products)
```

```yaml  theme={null}
compatibility: Requires git, docker, jq, and access to the internet
```

<Note>
  Most skills do not need the `compatibility` field.
</Note>

#### `metadata` field

The optional `metadata` field:

* A map from string keys to string values
* Clients can use this to store additional properties not defined by the Agent Skills spec
* We recommend making your key names reasonably unique to avoid accidental conflicts

Example:

```yaml  theme={null}
metadata:
  author: example-org
  version: "1.0"
```

#### `allowed-tools` field

The optional `allowed-tools` field:

* A space-delimited list of tools that are pre-approved to run
* Experimental. Support for this field may vary between agent implementations

Example:

```yaml  theme={null}
allowed-tools: Bash(git:*) Bash(jq:*) Read
```

### Body content

The Markdown body after the frontmatter contains the skill instructions. There are no format restrictions. Write whatever helps agents perform the task effectively.

Recommended sections:

* Step-by-step instructions
* Examples of inputs and outputs
* Common edge cases

Note that the agent will load this entire file once it's decided to activate a skill. Consider splitting longer `SKILL.md` content into referenced files.

## Optional directories

### scripts/

Contains executable code that agents can run. Scripts should:

* Be self-contained or clearly document dependencies
* Include helpful error messages
* Handle edge cases gracefully

Supported languages depend on the agent implementation. Common options include Python, Bash, and JavaScript.

### references/

Contains additional documentation that agents can read when needed:

* `REFERENCE.md` - Detailed technical reference
* `FORMS.md` - Form templates or structured data formats
* Domain-specific files (`finance.md`, `legal.md`, etc.)

Keep individual [reference files](#file-references) focused. Agents load these on demand, so smaller files mean less use of context.

### assets/

Contains static resources:

* Templates (document templates, configuration templates)
* Images (diagrams, examples)
* Data files (lookup tables, schemas)

## Progressive disclosure

Skills should be structured for efficient use of context:

1. **Metadata** (\~100 tokens): The `name` and `description` fields are loaded at startup for all skills
2. **Instructions** (\< 5000 tokens recommended): The full `SKILL.md` body is loaded when the skill is activated
3. **Resources** (as needed): Files (e.g. those in `scripts/`, `references/`, or `assets/`) are loaded only when required

Keep your main `SKILL.md` under 500 lines. Move detailed reference material to separate files.

## File references

When referencing other files in your skill, use relative paths from the skill root:

```markdown  theme={null}
See [the reference guide](references/REFERENCE.md) for details.

Run the extraction script:
scripts/extract.py
```

Keep file references one level deep from `SKILL.md`. Avoid deeply nested reference chains.

## Validation

Use the [skills-ref](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills/tree/main/skills-ref) reference library to validate your skills:

```bash  theme={null}
skills-ref validate ./my-skill
```

This checks that your `SKILL.md` frontmatter is valid and follows all naming conventions.

# Integrate skills into your agent

> How to add Agent Skills support to your agent or tool.

This guide explains how to add skills support to an AI agent or development tool.

## Integration approaches

The two main approaches to integrating skills are:

**Filesystem-based agents** operate within a computer environment (bash/unix) and represent the most capable option. Skills are activated when models issue shell commands like `cat /path/to/my-skill/SKILL.md`. Bundled resources are accessed through shell commands.

**Tool-based agents** function without a dedicated computer environment. Instead, they implement tools allowing models to trigger skills and access bundled assets. The specific tool implementation is up to the developer.

## Overview

A skills-compatible agent needs to:

1. **Discover** skills in configured directories
2. **Load metadata** (name and description) at startup
3. **Match** user tasks to relevant skills
4. **Activate** skills by loading full instructions
5. **Execute** scripts and access resources as needed

## Skill discovery

Skills are folders containing a `SKILL.md` file. Your agent should scan configured directories for valid skills.

## Loading metadata

At startup, parse only the frontmatter of each `SKILL.md` file. This keeps initial context usage low.

### Parsing frontmatter

```
function parseMetadata(skillPath):
    content = readFile(skillPath + "/SKILL.md")
    frontmatter = extractYAMLFrontmatter(content)

    return {
        name: frontmatter.name,
        description: frontmatter.description,
        path: skillPath
    }
```

### Injecting into context

Include skill metadata in the system prompt so the model knows what skills are available.

Follow your platform's guidance for system prompt updates. For example, for Claude models, the recommended format uses XML:

```xml  theme={null}
<available_skills>
  <skill>
    <name>pdf-processing</name>
    <description>Extracts text and tables from PDF files, fills forms, merges documents.</description>
    <location>/path/to/skills/pdf-processing/SKILL.md</location>
  </skill>
  <skill>
    <name>data-analysis</name>
    <description>Analyzes datasets, generates charts, and creates summary reports.</description>
    <location>/path/to/skills/data-analysis/SKILL.md</location>
  </skill>
</available_skills>
```

For filesystem-based agents, include the `location` field with the absolute path to the SKILL.md file. For tool-based agents, the location can be omitted.

Keep metadata concise. Each skill should add roughly 50-100 tokens to the context.

## Security considerations

Script execution introduces security risks. Consider:

* **Sandboxing**: Run scripts in isolated environments
* **Allowlisting**: Only execute scripts from trusted skills
* **Confirmation**: Ask users before running potentially dangerous operations
* **Logging**: Record all script executions for auditing

## Reference implementation

The [skills-ref](https://github.com/agentskills/agentskills/tree/main/skills-ref) library provides Python utilities and a CLI for working with skills.

For example:

**Validate a skill directory:**

```
skills-ref validate <path>
```

**Generate `<available_skills>` XML for agent prompts:**

```
skills-ref to-prompt <path>...
```

Use the library source code as a reference implementation.

```
import { isAbsolute, resolve } from 'path';
import type { ParsedSource } from './types.ts';

/**
 * Extract owner/repo (or group/subgroup/repo for GitLab) from a parsed source
 * for lockfile tracking and telemetry.
 * Returns null for local paths or unparseable sources.
 * Supports any Git host with an owner/repo URL structure, including GitLab subgroups.
 */
export function getOwnerRepo(parsed: ParsedSource): string | null {
  if (parsed.type === 'local') {
    return null;
  }

  // Only handle HTTP(S) URLs
  if (!parsed.url.startsWith('http://') && !parsed.url.startsWith('https://')) {
    return null;
  }

  try {
    const url = new URL(parsed.url);
    // Get pathname, remove leading slash and trailing .git
    let path = url.pathname.slice(1);
    path = path.replace(/\.git$/, '');

    // Must have at least owner/repo (one slash)
    if (path.includes('/')) {
      return path;
    }
  } catch {
    // Invalid URL
  }

  return null;
}

/**
 * Extract owner and repo from an owner/repo string.
 * Returns null if the format is invalid.
 */
export function parseOwnerRepo(ownerRepo: string): { owner: string; repo: string } | null {
  const match = ownerRepo.match(/^([^/]+)\/([^/]+)$/);
  if (match) {
    return { owner: match[1]!, repo: match[2]! };
  }
  return null;
}

 * Check if a GitHub repository is private.

---
name: pdf
description: Use this skill whenever the user wants to do anything with PDF files. This includes reading or extracting text/tables from PDFs, combining or merging multiple PDFs into one, splitting PDFs apart, rotating pages, adding watermarks, creating new PDFs, filling PDF forms, encrypting/decrypting PDFs, extracting images, and OCR on scanned PDFs to make them searchable. If the user mentions a .pdf file or asks to produce one, use this skill.
license: Proprietary. LICENSE.txt has complete terms
---

# PDF Processing Guide

## Overview

This guide covers essential PDF processing operations using Python libraries and command-line tools. For advanced features, JavaScript libraries, and detailed examples, see REFERENCE.md. If you need to fill out a PDF form, read FORMS.md and follow its instructions.

## Quick Start

```python
from pypdf import PdfReader, PdfWriter

# Read a PDF
reader = PdfReader("document.pdf")
print(f"Pages: {len(reader.pages)}")

# Extract text
text = ""
for page in reader.pages:
    text += page.extract_text()
```

## Python Libraries

### pypdf - Basic Operations

#### Merge PDFs
```python
from pypdf import PdfWriter, PdfReader

writer = PdfWriter()
for pdf_file in ["doc1.pdf", "doc2.pdf", "doc3.pdf"]:
    reader = PdfReader(pdf_file)
    for page in reader.pages:
        writer.add_page(page)

with open("merged.pdf", "wb") as output:
    writer.write(output)
```

#### Split PDF
```python
reader = PdfReader("input.pdf")
for i, page in enumerate(reader.pages):
    writer = PdfWriter()
    writer.add_page(page)
    with open(f"page_{i+1}.pdf", "wb") as output:
        writer.write(output)
```

#### Extract Metadata
```python
reader = PdfReader("document.pdf")
meta = reader.metadata
print(f"Title: {meta.title}")
print(f"Author: {meta.author}")
print(f"Subject: {meta.subject}")
print(f"Creator: {meta.creator}")
```

#### Rotate Pages
```python
reader = PdfReader("input.pdf")
writer = PdfWriter()

page = reader.pages[0]
page.rotate(90)  # Rotate 90 degrees clockwise
writer.add_page(page)

with open("rotated.pdf", "wb") as output:
    writer.write(output)
```

### pdfplumber - Text and Table Extraction

#### Extract Text with Layout
```python
import pdfplumber

with pdfplumber.open("document.pdf") as pdf:
    for page in pdf.pages:
        text = page.extract_text()
        print(text)
```

#### Extract Tables
```python
with pdfplumber.open("document.pdf") as pdf:
    for i, page in enumerate(pdf.pages):
        tables = page.extract_tables()
        for j, table in enumerate(tables):
            print(f"Table {j+1} on page {i+1}:")
            for row in table:
                print(row)
```

{
  description = "Declarative Agent Skills management with flake-pinned sources and Home Manager integration";

  inputs = {
    nixpkgs.url = "github:NixOS/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable";
    home-manager = {
      url = "github:nix-community/home-manager";
      inputs.nixpkgs.follows = "nixpkgs";
    };
  };

  outputs = inputs@{ self, nixpkgs, home-manager, ... }:
    let
      systems = [
        "x86_64-linux"
        "aarch64-linux"
        "x86_64-darwin"
        "aarch64-darwin"
      ];

      forAllSystems = nixpkgs.lib.genAttrs systems;
      lib = import ./lib {
        lib = nixpkgs.lib;
        inherit inputs;
      };

      # Default global targets are defined in lib/default.nix; see README.md#default-target-paths.
      defaultTargets = lib.defaultTargets;

      # Default local targets are defined in lib/default.nix; see README.md#default-target-paths.
      defaultLocalTargets = lib.defaultLocalTargets;

      defaultConfig = {
        # Add sources and skills (enable/explicit) in your consumer flake; kept empty here to stay neutral.
        sources = {};
        skills = {
          enable = [];
          enableAll = false;
          explicit = {};
        };
        targets = defaultTargets;
        excludePatterns = lib.defaultExcludePatterns;
      };

      defaultCatalog = lib.discoverCatalog defaultConfig.sources;
      defaultAllowlist = lib.allowlistFor {
        catalog = defaultCatalog;
        sources = defaultConfig.sources;
        enableAll = defaultConfig.skills.enableAll;
        enable = defaultConfig.skills.enable;
      };
      defaultSelection = lib.selectSkills {
        catalog = defaultCatalog;
        allowlist = defaultAllowlist;
        skills = defaultConfig.skills.explicit;
        sources = defaultConfig.sources;
      };

      bundleFor = system:
        let pkgs = nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}; in
        lib.mkBundle { inherit pkgs; selection = defaultSelection; name = "agent-skills-bundle"; };
    in
    {
      formatter = forAllSystems (system: nixpkgs.legacyPackages.${system}.nixpkgs-fmt);

      packages = forAllSystems (system: let

        agent-skills-bundle = bundle;
        default = bundle;

          listJson = pkgs.writeText "agent-skills-catalog.json" (builtins.toJSON (lib.catalogJson defaultCatalog));

```
# agent-skills-nix

Declarative management of Agent Skills (directories containing `SKILL.md`) with flake-pinned sources, discovery, selection, bundling, and Home Manager integration.

## Concepts

- **sources**: Named inputs (flake or path) pointing at a skills root (`subdir`).
- **discover**: Recursively scans sources for directories that contain `SKILL.md`, producing a catalog. Skills can be nested (e.g. `ecosystem/c-ecosystem/`) and their IDs use `/` as separator.
- **skills.enable / skills.enableAll / skills.explicit**: Declaratively pick discovered skills, enable-all (global or by source list), and explicitly specified ones; no accidental auto-install unless you opt in.
- **targets**: Agent-specific destinations synced from a store bundle (structure: `link`, `symlink-tree`, `copy-tree`). Targets are opt-in (`enable = false` by default). The `dest` option supports shell variable expansion at runtime (e.g. `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/skills`). See **Default target paths** below.

## Source filters

Each source supports optional filters to control discovery:

- **`filter.maxDepth`** (`null | int`, default: `null`): Maximum recursion depth for SKILL.md discovery. `null` = unlimited (capped internally at 100 to guard against symlink loops), `1` = immediate children only, `2` = one level of nesting. Set to `1` to restore pre-recursive (flat-only) behavior.
- **`filter.nameRegex`** (`null | string`, default: `null`): Regex matched against the skill's relative path (e.g. `cat-a/skill-1`) to restrict discovery.

## Default target paths

| Target | Global path | Local path |
|--------|-------------|------------|
| agents | `$HOME/.agents/skills` | `.agents/skills` |
| codex | `${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills` | `.codex/skills` |
| claude | `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/skills` | `.claude/skills` |
| copilot | `$HOME/.copilot/skills` | `.github/skills` |
| cursor | `$HOME/.cursor/skills` | `.cursor/skills` |
| windsurf | `$HOME/.codeium/windsurf/skills` | `.windsurf/skills` |
| antigravity | `$HOME/.gemini/antigravity/skills` | `.agent/skills` |
| gemini | `$HOME/.gemini/skills` | `.gemini/skills` |

## Quick start (child flake + Home Manager)

Put skills config in a small child flake so the only pinned inputs there are skill sources.

Use the quickstart example:

- Overview: [`examples/quickstart/README.md`](./examples/quickstart/README.md)
- Main (tightly coupled): [`examples/quickstart/main/flake.nix`](./examples/quickstart/main/flake.nix)
- Child (separated catalog): [`examples/quickstart/child/flake.nix`](./examples/quickstart/child/flake.nix)

Notes:

- In `main`, `agent-skills` and skill sources are listed directly in the top-level inputs.
- In `child`, top-level only depends on `skills-catalog = path:./skills`; skills inputs live under `./skills/flake.nix`.
- If you use source `input` references in your module config, pass flake `inputs` to Home Manager via `extraSpecialArgs`.
- To enable a default target, set `targets.<name>.enable = true;` (e.g. `targets.claude.enable = true;`).
- `structure = "link"` uses `home.file` symlinks; `symlink-tree` and `copy-tree` run in `home.activation`.
- `symlink-tree` uses `rsync -a --delete` (preserve symlinks); `copy-tree` uses `rsync -aL --delete` (dereference symlinks).
- `dest` supports shell variable expansion at runtime (e.g. `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/skills`). Note: `link` structure does not support shell variables and will use the fallback path.

## Flake outputs

- `packages.<system>.agent-skills-bundle`: Store bundle of selected skills (empty by default; configure in consumers).
- `apps.<system>.skills-install`: Sync bundle to enabled global targets (see **Default target paths**). Override destinations with `AGENT_SKILLS_DESTS`.
- `apps.<system>.skills-install-local`: Sync bundle to enabled local targets (see **Default target paths**) using `copy-tree`. Override root with `AGENT_SKILLS_ROOT`, destinations with `AGENT_SKILLS_LOCAL_DESTS`.
- `apps.<system>.skills-list`: JSON view of the default catalog.
- `checks.<system>.skills`: Sanity check that the bundle builds.
- `homeManagerModules.default`: Home Manager module implementing the DSL above.
- `lib.agent-skills`: Helper functions (`discoverCatalog`, `selectSkills`, `mkBundle`, `mkLocalInstallScript`, `mkShellHook`, `catalogJson`, `defaultConfig`).

## Library functions

See [`examples/library-functions/snippet.nix`](./examples/library-functions/snippet.nix).

`discoverCatalog` recursively discovers `SKILL.md` directories and generates `/`-separated IDs for nested skills (e.g. `cat-a/skill-1`). It enforces `SKILL.md` presence and rejects duplicate IDs (error messages include absolute paths for both conflicting sources). `selectSkills` errors on unknown allowlist entries or missing files, preventing accidental drift. (Home Manager maps `skills.enable` → `allowlist` and `skills.explicit` → `skills`.)

## Skill customisation

Explicit skills support `transform` and `packages` options to customise SKILL.md and bundle dependencies:

See [`examples/skill-customization/explicit-transform.nix`](./examples/skill-customization/explicit-transform.nix).

This generates:

```
my-skill/
├── SKILL.md
├── jq -> /nix/store/xxx-jq/bin/jq
└── curl/ -> /nix/store/xxx-curl/bin/  (for packages with multiple binaries)
```

With SKILL.md containing the transformed content.

**Transform function arguments:**
- `original`: The original SKILL.md content
- `dependencies`: A markdown table of package dependencies with local paths (e.g., `./jq`)

**Default behaviour (no transform):**
- If only `packages` is specified, the default is `dependencies + original`
- If neither is specified, the original SKILL.md is used as-is

Package binaries are referenced with local paths (`./jq` or `./pkg/` for multi-binary packages) to reduce context consumption when agents load the skill.

## Apps usage

### Global skills (Home Manager)

- List catalog: `nix run .#skills-list`
- Sync bundle to `$HOME`: `nix run .#skills-install` (override destinations via `AGENT_SKILLS_DESTS="~/tmp/skills1 ~/tmp/skills2"`)

### Local skills (project-local)

- Sync bundle to current directory: `nix run .#skills-install-local`

Local skills are installed to enabled local targets in **Default target paths** relative to the current working directory (or `AGENT_SKILLS_ROOT` if set). Override destinations via `AGENT_SKILLS_LOCAL_DESTS`.
Targets respect `enable`, `systems`, and `structure` (default `copy-tree`). To exclude a target, disable it or provide custom targets to `mkLocalInstallScript`.
Local install skips non-Nix-managed existing paths to avoid clobbering user data; set `AGENT_SKILLS_FORCE=1` to overwrite.

Both apps operate on the flake's default (empty) config; point at your own flake/module for real catalogs.

## Local skills in your project

To install skills locally in your project, use `mkLocalInstallScript` in your flake:

See [`examples/local-install/flake.nix`](./examples/local-install/flake.nix).

Then run `nix run .#skills-install-local` from your project root to install skills to enabled local targets in **Default target paths**.

### Auto-install with devShell

Use `mkShellHook` to automatically install skills when entering a dev shell:

See [`examples/devshell/flake.nix`](./examples/devshell/flake.nix).

Now `nix develop` will automatically install skills to your project directory.

## Checks / safety

- Disallows skill IDs containing `/..` or leading `/`.
- Verifies `SKILL.md` for discovered and explicit skills.
- Fails on duplicate IDs across sources.
- Caps recursion at 100 levels when maxDepth is null to guard against symlink loops.
- Activation scripts always `mkdir -p` and use `rsync -a --delete` by default.

## Breaking changes

### filter.maxDepth default changed from 1 to null

Skill discovery now recurses into nested directories by default. If your source layout relies on flat-only discovery (one level of directories under the source root), add `filter.maxDepth = 1;` to your source configuration:

```nix
sources.my-skills = {
  path = ./skills;
  filter.maxDepth = 1;  # restore flat-only behavior
};
```
```

  "content": "# Agent Skills\n\n## Summary\n\nrepiq を Agent Skills 標準フォーマットで公開し、Claude Code・Cursor・Gemini CLI 等のスキル対応エージェントから直接利用可能にする。\n\n## Background & Purpose\n\nrepiq は「AI エージェントが OSS リポジトリ・ライブラリを選定するための客観メトリクス CLI」として設計されている。現在エージェントが repiq を使うには CLI をサブプロセスとして実行する必要があり、以下の課題がある:\n\n- エージェントが repiq の存在や使い方を知らない (発見性の問題)\n- CLI のインストール・セットアップが必要 (導入障壁)\n- コマンド引数やスキーム形式をエージェントが正しく組み立てる保証がない (信頼性)\n\nAgent Skills はエージェントに「いつ・どう使うか」を宣言的に伝える標準フォーマットであり、これを採用することで上記の課題をすべて解決できる。\n\n## Why Now\n\n- Agent Skills が Anthropic 発の標準として確立され、Claude Code・Cursor・Gemini CLI・GitHub Copilot 等 30+ のエージェント製品が対応済み\n- repiq は 5 プロバイダー (GitHub, npm, PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules) の実装が完了し、スキルとして提供するに十分な機能が揃った\n- agent-skills-nix による Nix ベースの宣言的配布も成熟し、Nix ユーザーへのリーチも可能\n\n## Hypothesis\n\n- H1: If we publish repiq as an Agent Skill, then agents (Claude Code, Cursor etc.) will automatically discover and use repiq when users ask about library selection or repository evaluation\n- H2: If we provide clear instructions in SKILL.md, then agents will correctly construct repiq commands and interpret the JSON output without user intervention\n- H3: If we distribute via both `npx skills add` and agent-skills-nix, then adoption friction drops to near zero for both npm and Nix users\n\n## Expected Outcome\n\n- repiq が Agent Skills 対応エージェントのエコシステムに参加し、ユーザーが意識せずとも repiq のデータをエージェント経由で利用可能になる\n- ライブラリ選定タスクで repiq のメトリクスが自動的に参照され、データに基づく意思決定が促進される\n- repiq の GitHub リポジトリへのトラフィック・Star が増加する\n"
}
```

  "content": "# Scope: Agent Skills\n\n## In Scope\n\n- repiq 用の SKILL.md ファイル作成 (Agent Skills 標準フォーマット準拠)\n- 全 5 プロバイダー (GitHub, npm, PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules) の使い方をカバー\n- `skills/repiq/` ディレクトリとして repiq リポジトリ内に配置\n- `npx skills add github:yutakobayashidev/repiq` でのインストール対応\n- agent-skills-nix での宣言的インストール対応 (dotnix への設定追加)\n- skills-ref による SKILL.md バリデーション通過\n\n## Out of Scope\n\n- MCP Server としての実装 (別エピック)\n- 新しいプロバイダーの追加 (既存 5 プロバイダーのみ対象)\n- repiq の CLI インターフェース変更 (現行 CLI をそのまま利用)\n- エージェント側の実装変更 (標準フォーマットに従うだけ)\n- スキルの有料配布・ライセンス制限\n\n## Success Criteria (KPI)\n\n### Expected to Improve\n\n- repiq の発見性: Agent Skills 対応エージェントからの自然な利用開始\n- 導入障壁の低減: `npx skills add` / `nix run` によるワンコマンドインストール\n- エージェントの repiq 利用成功率: SKILL.md の指示に従った正確なコマンド実行\n\n### At Risk (may decrease)\n\n- 特になし (既存 CLI に変更を加えないため副作用リスクは低い)\n\n## Acceptance Gates\n\n- [ ] `skills/repiq/SKILL.md` が Agent Skills 仕様に準拠している (skills-ref validate 通過)\n- [ ] `npx skills add github:yutakobayashidev/repiq` でインストール可能\n- [ ] Claude Code でスキルが発見・発動し、`repiq github:<repo>` 等が正しく実行される\n- [ ] agent-skills-nix 経由で Nix 環境にインストール可能\n- [ ] SKILL.md が 500 行以内に収まっている (progressive disclosure 準拠)\n\n## Experiment Info (if applicable)\n\n- N/A (実験ではなく、標準フォーマットへの対応)\n"
}

```
     1→# Agent Skills
     2→
     3→## Summary
     4→
     5→repiq を Agent Skills 標準フォーマットで公開し、Claude Code・Cursor・Gemini CLI 等のスキル対応エージェントから直接利用可能にする。
     6→
     7→## Background & Purpose
     8→
     9→repiq は「AI エージェントが OSS リポジトリ・ライブラリを選定するための客観メトリクス CLI」として設計されている。現在エージェントが repiq を使うには CLI をサブプロセスとして実行する必要があり、以下の課題がある:
    10→
    11→- エージェントが repiq の存在や使い方を知らない (発見性の問題)
    12→- CLI のインストール・セットアップが必要 (導入障壁)
    13→- コマンド引数やスキーム形式をエージェントが正しく組み立てる保証がない (信頼性)
    14→
    15→Agent Skills はエージェントに「いつ・どう使うか」を宣言的に伝える標準フォーマットであり、これを採用することで上記の課題をすべて解決できる。
    16→
    17→## Why Now
    18→
    19→- Agent Skills が Anthropic 発の標準として確立され、Claude Code・Cursor・Gemini CLI・GitHub Copilot 等 30+ のエージェント製品が対応済み
    20→- repiq は 5 プロバイダー (GitHub, npm, PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules) の実装が完了し、スキルとして提供するに十分な機能が揃った
    21→- agent-skills-nix による Nix ベースの宣言的配布も成熟し、Nix ユーザーへのリーチも可能
    22→
    23→## Hypothesis
    24→
    25→- H1: If we publish repiq as an Agent Skill, then agents (Claude Code, Cursor etc.) will automatically discover and use repiq when users ask about library selection or repository evaluation
    26→- H2: If we provide clear instructions in SKILL.md, then agents will correctly construct repiq commands and interpret the JSON output without user intervention
    27→- H3: If we distribute via both `npx skills add` and agent-skills-nix, then adoption friction drops to near zero for both npm and Nix users
    28→

    30→

Perfect! Now I have comprehensive information. Let me create a detailed report of the codebase investigation.

## Thorough Investigation Report: repiq Codebase

I've completed a comprehensive investigation of the repiq codebase. Here are all the findings to inform Agent Skills feature spec design:

### 1. CLI Interface (`internal/cli/cli.go`)

**Flags:**
- `--json` (default) - Output as JSON array
- `--ndjson` - Output as newline-delimited JSON
- `--markdown` - Output as Markdown table
- `--no-cache` - Bypass cache and always fetch from API
- `--version` - Print version and exit
- `--help` - Print help (implicit, from flag package)

**Input Format:**
- `<scheme>:<identifier>` pattern
- Multiple targets supported: `repiq github:owner/repo npm:package pypi:pkg crates:crate go:module`
- Parsed via `provider.ParseTarget()` which validates:
  - Colon separator presence
  - Non-empty scheme
  - Non-empty identifier

**Output Behavior:**
- Multiple targets fetched in parallel using goroutines
- Timeout: 30 seconds globally for all fetches
- Exit code: 0 on all success, 1 if ANY target fails
- Errors returned in Result.Error field (not stderr), allowing partial success

**Flag Priority (format):**
When multiple format flags set: markdown > ndjson > json (last specified wins)

---

### 2. Provider Registry & Schemes (`internal/provider/registry.go` and `target.go`)

**Registered Providers:**
| Scheme | Provider Name | Identifier Format | File |
|--------|---------------|-------------------|------|
| `github` | GitHub API | `owner/repo` | `internal/provider/github/` |
| `npm` | npm registry | `package` or `@scope/package` | `internal/provider/npm/` |
| `pypi` | PyPI registry | `package` | `internal/provider/pypi/` |
| `crates` | crates.io API | `crate` | `internal/provider/crates/` |
| `go` | Go Module Proxy + deps.dev | `module.path/sub` | `internal/provider/golang/` |

**Validation Rules:**
- GitHub: `owner/repo` format, names match `[a-zA-Z0-9._-]+`
- npm: `^(@[a-zA-Z0-9][\w.-]*/)?[a-zA-Z0-9][\w.-]*$` (supports scoped packages)
- PyPI: `^[a-zA-Z0-9]([a-zA-Z0-9._-]*[a-zA-Z0-9])?$`
- crates: `^[a-zA-Z0-9][a-zA-Z0-9_-]*$`
- Go: `^[a-zA-Z0-9][-a-zA-Z0-9.]*\.[a-zA-Z]{2,}/.+$` (requires domain and path)

---

### 3. Output Formats (`internal/format/format.go`)

#### JSON Format
- Single JSON array of Result objects
- Indented (2 spaces)
- Ends with newline

Example structure:
```json
[
  {
    "target": "github:facebook/react",
    "github": {
      "stars": 215000,
      "forks": 45000,
      ...
    }
  },
  {
    "target": "npm:react",
    "npm": {
      "weekly_downloads": 25000000,
      ...
    }
  }
]
```

#### NDJSON Format
- One JSON object per line
- No indentation
- No surrounding array brackets

#### Markdown Format
- **Provider-specific tables** (separated by blank lines)
- Each table has columns for target, provider-specific metrics, and error
- Pipes escaped in values: `|` → `\|`
- Tables grouped by provider type in order: GitHub, npm, PyPI, crates, Go, then errors

---

### 4. Result & Metrics Structs (`internal/provider/provider.go`)

**Result** (unified output container):
```go
type Result struct {
  Target string              // "scheme:identifier"
  GitHub *GitHubMetrics      // Only GitHub provider fills this
  NPM    *NPMMetrics         // Only npm provider fills this
  PyPI   *PyPIMetrics        // Only PyPI provider fills this
  Crates *CratesMetrics      // Only crates provider fills this
  Go     *GoMetrics          // Only Go provider fills this
  Error  string              // Set if fetch failed
}
```

**GitHubMetrics** (8 fields):
- `stars` - int
- `forks` - int
- `open_issues` - int (includes PRs per GitHub API)
- `contributors` - int
- `release_count` - int
- `last_commit_days` - int
- `commits_30d` - int
- `issues_closed_30d` - int

**NPMMetrics** (5 fields):
- `weekly_downloads` - int
- `latest_version` - string
- `last_publish_days` - int
- `dependencies_count` - int
- `license` - string

**PyPIMetrics** (7 fields):
- `weekly_downloads` - int
- `monthly_downloads` - int
- `latest_version` - string
- `last_publish_days` - int
- `dependencies_count` - int
- `license` - string
- `requires_python` - string (e.g., ">=3.9")

**CratesMetrics** (7 fields):
- `downloads` - int (all-time)
- `recent_downloads` - int (90 days)
- `latest_version` - string
- `last_publish_days` - int
- `dependencies_count` - int
- `license` - string
- `reverse_dependencies` - int

**GoMetrics** (4 fields):
- `latest_version` - string
- `last_publish_days` - int
- `dependencies_count` - int
- `license` - string

---

### 5. Authentication (`internal/auth/auth.go`)

**Token Resolution (GitHub only):**
1. Try `gh auth token` command
2. Fall back to `GITHUB_TOKEN` environment variable
3. Fall back to unauthenticated (empty token)

**Resolver struct:**
```go
type Resolver struct {
  Cmd    CmdRunner      // Abstracted for testing
  Getenv func(string) string
}
```

**Usage in CLI:**
```go
resolver := &auth.Resolver{
  Cmd:    auth.ExecRunner{},
  Getenv: os.Getenv,
}
token := resolver.ResolveToken()
```

**Impact:**
- Authenticated: 5,000 req/hour rate limit
- Unauthenticated: 60 req/hour rate limit

---

### 6. Caching (`internal/cache/provider.go` and `store.go`)

**Store Location:** `~/.cache/repiq/` (via `os.UserCacheDir()`)

**Cache Key:** SHA-256 hash of `scheme:identifier`
- File format: `{hash}.json`
- Example: `5feceb66ffc86f38d952786c6d696c79c2dbc238c4cafb11f2271d7a122370f.json`

**TTL:** 24 hours

**Cache Entry Structure:**
```json
{
  "cached_at": "2026-02-25T10:42:20Z",
  "result": { /* full Result object */ }
}
```

**Decorator Pattern:**
- `cache.NewProvider()` wraps any provider
- `--no-cache` flag bypasses reads but still writes (subsequent runs benefit)
- Applied to all 5 providers in CLI init

---

### 7. Example JSON Output (from test fixtures)

**Single GitHub result:**
```json
[
  {
    "target": "github:facebook/react",
    "github": {
      "stars": 215000,
      "forks": 45000,
      "open_issues": 1000,
      "contributors": 1500,
      "release_count": 200,
      "last_commit_days": 1,
      "commits_30d": 120,
      "issues_closed_30d": 340
    }
  }
]
```

**Mixed providers (partial):**
```json
[
  {
    "target": "github:facebook/react",
    "github": { /* 8 metrics */ }
  },
  {
    "target": "npm:react",
    "npm": {
      "weekly_downloads": 25000000,
      "latest_version": "19.1.0",
      "last_publish_days": 15,
      "dependencies_count": 2,
      "license": "MIT"
    }
  },
  {
    "target": "pypi:requests",
    "pypi": {
      "weekly_downloads": 5000000,
      "monthly_downloads": 20000000,
      "latest_version": "2.31.0",
      "last_publish_days": 30,
      "dependencies_count": 5,
      "license": "Apache-2.0",
      "requires_python": ">=3.7"
    }
  },
  {
    "target": "crates:serde",
    "crates": {
      "downloads": 80000000,
      "recent_downloads": 3000000,
      "latest_version": "1.0.197",
      "last_publish_days": 7,
      "dependencies_count": 2,
      "license": "MIT OR Apache-2.0",
      "reverse_dependencies": 30000
    }
  },
  {
    "target": "go:github.com/gin-gonic/gin",
    "go": {
      "latest_version": "v1.9.1",
      "last_publish_days": 60,
      "dependencies_count": 10,
      "license": "MIT"
    }
  },
  {
    "target": "github:nonexistent/repo",
    "error": "GitHub API: 404 Not Found"
  }
]
```

---

### 8. README.md Usage Examples

```bash
# Single repo (JSON default)
repiq github:facebook/react

# npm package
repiq npm:react

# Scoped npm package
repiq npm:@types/node

# Python package (PyPI)
repiq pypi:requests

# Rust crate (crates.io)
repiq crates:serde

# Go module
repiq go:golang.org/x/text

# Multiple targets in NDJSON
repiq --ndjson github:facebook/react github:vuejs/core

# Mixed providers in Markdown table
repiq --markdown github:facebook/react npm:react pypi:flask crates:tokio go:golang.org/x/net
```

---

### 9. Architecture Highlights

**Provider Interface:**
```go
type Provider interface {
  Scheme() string
  Fetch(ctx context.Context, identifier string) (Result, error)
}
```

**Key Design Patterns:**
1. **Registry Pattern** - Dynamic provider lookup by scheme
2. **Decorator Pattern** - Cache wraps providers without changing their interface
3. **Parallel Fetching** - sync.WaitGroup for concurrent API calls
4. **Unified Result Format** - All providers return same Result struct (provider-specific metrics nil)
5. **Error Handling** - Errors stored in Result.Error (not panics), allowing partial success

**Concurrency:**
- Multiple targets fetched in parallel goroutines
- Each provider may also fetch metrics in parallel (e.g., GitHub fetches 5 separate API calls)
- Timeout: 30 seconds for entire operation

---

### 10. External Dependencies

**Go Dependencies:**
- `github.com/google/go-github/v68` - GitHub API client (google/go-github)
- Standard library: `flag`, `net/http`, `context`, `time`, `encoding/json`, etc.

**External APIs Used:**
- GitHub: https://api.github.com (with optional token auth)
- npm: https://registry.npmjs.org, https://api.npmjs.org
- PyPI: https://pypi.org, https://pypistats.org
- crates.io: https://crates.io
- Go Module Proxy: https://proxy.golang.org, https://api.deps.dev

---

### 11. Testing Examples

From `internal/format/format_test.go`, test fixtures show all provider outputs, including:
- Mixed provider results
- Error handling
- Empty results
- Markdown table separation by provider type

---

### Summary for Agent Skills Spec

An agent using repiq should know:
1. **CLI invocation:** `repiq [flags] <scheme>:<id> [...]`
2. **Available schemes & formats:** 5 providers, 3 output formats
3. **Output structure:** Unified Result with provider-specific metrics (one per Result)
4. **Error handling:** Partial success possible; errors in Result.Error field
5. **Metrics vary by provider:** GitHub has 8, npm/PyPI/Go have 4-7, crates has 7
6. **Authentication:** GitHub only; auto-resolved from `gh auth token` or `GITHUB_TOKEN`
7. **Caching:** Automatic 24h cache; `--no-cache` to bypass
8. **Performance:** ~3 seconds per repo typical, 30 second timeout
9. **Parallel execution:** Multiple targets fetched simultaneously

---

**All findings are from actual source code in `/home/yuta/ghq/github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/`**

{
  "file_path": "/home/yuta/ghq/github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/.git/wt/docs/agent-skills/docs/specs/agent-skills/skill/requirements.md",
  "content": "# Requirements: SKILL.md 作成\n\n## Functional Requirements\n\n### P1 (Must have)\n\n- `skills/repiq/SKILL.md` を Agent Skills 仕様に準拠して作成する\n  - frontmatter: `name`, `description` (必須フィールド)\n  - `name` は `repiq` (ディレクトリ名と一致)\n  - `description` はエージェントが「いつこのスキルを発動すべきか」を判断できるトリガーキーワードを含む\n- SKILL.md の本文に以下を含める:\n  - repiq の目的 (データのみ提供、判断はしない)\n  - CLI の呼び出し形式: `repiq [flags] <scheme>:<identifier> [...]`\n  - 全 5 スキームの形式と例: `github:owner/repo`, `npm:package`, `pypi:package`, `crates:crate`, `go:module/path`\n  - 出力フォーマットフラグ: `--json` (デフォルト), `--ndjson`, `--markdown`\n  - `--no-cache` フラグの説明\n  - 代表的なユースケース (ライブラリ比較、リポジトリ健全性評価)\n- `skills/repiq/references/REFERENCE.md` に以下を分離:\n  - 各プロバイダーの JSON 出力フィールド一覧と意味\n  - GitHubMetrics (8 fields), NPMMetrics (5), PyPIMetrics (7), CratesMetrics (7), GoMetrics (4)\n  - エラー出力の形式\n- SKILL.md から REFERENCE.md へ相対パスで参照する\n- `skills-ref validate ./skills/repiq` でバリデーションが通過する\n- `npx skills add github:yutakobayashidev/repiq` でインストール可能である (skills/ ディレクトリがリポジトリルートに存在すれば自動検出される)\n\n### P2 (Should have)\n\n- SKILL.md の `description` フィールドにエージェントが反応しやすいキーワードを含める:\n  - \"repository\", \"library\", \"package\", \"metrics\", \"stars\", \"downloads\", \"compare\", \"evaluate\", \"OSS\"\n- frontmatter に `license` フィールドを含める (MIT)\n- frontmatter に `compatibility` フィールドを含め、repiq CLI が PATH に必要な旨を記載\n- frontmatter に `metadata` (author, version) を含める\n- README.md に Agent Skills セクションを追加し、インストール方法を記載\n\n### P3 (Nice to have)\n\n- frontmatter の `allowed-tools` に `Bash(repiq:*)` を指定 (実験的フィールド)\n\n## Non-Functional Requirements\n\n- SKILL.md は 500 行以内 (progressive disclosure 準拠、推奨 5000 トークン以下)\n- REFERENCE.md は構造化されたテーブル形式で、エージェントがフィールド名・型・意味を正確に把握できる\n- 英語で記述 (Agent Skills は国際標準フォーマットのため)\n\n## Edge Cases\n\n1. repiq が PATH に存在しない場合 → SKILL.md にインストール方法 (`go install` / Nix) を記載し、エージェントがユーザーに案内できるようにする\n2. GitHub 認証なしで実行した場合 → SKILL.md に rate limit の注意と `gh auth login` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` 設定を記載\n3. 存在しないパッケージ・リポジトリを指定した場合 → JSON 出力の `error` フィールドの解釈方法を REFERENCE.md に記載\n4. 複数ターゲット混在時 (github + npm + pypi) → 並列取得されること、結果が配列で返ることを明記\n\n## Constraints\n\n- 既存の repiq CLI に変更を加えない (SKILL.md とドキュメントのみ)\n- Agent Skills 仕様 (agentskills.io/specification) に厳密に準拠する\n- `name` フィールドはディレクトリ名 `repiq` と一致させる\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/yuta/ghq/github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/.git/wt/docs/agent-skills/docs/specs/agent-skills/skill/design.md",
  "content": "# Design: SKILL.md 作成\n\n## Current State\n\n- repiq は Go 製 CLI として動作し、5 プロバイダー (GitHub, npm, PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules) のメトリクスを JSON/NDJSON/Markdown で出力する\n- Agent Skills 対応はなく、エージェントが repiq を利用するには CLI の存在と使い方を事前に知っている必要がある\n- README.md に CLI の使用方法は記載されているが、Agent Skills フォーマットではない\n\n## Proposed Changes\n\n### 1. ディレクトリ構成\n\n```\nskills/\n└── repiq/\n    ├── SKILL.md              # メインスキルファイル\n    └── references/\n        └── REFERENCE.md      # 詳細出力スキーマ\n```\n\n### 2. SKILL.md 構成\n\n**Frontmatter:**\n\n```yaml\nname: repiq\ndescription: >-\n  Fetch objective metrics for OSS repositories and packages.\n  Use when evaluating, comparing, or selecting libraries and repositories.\n  Supports GitHub repos, npm/PyPI/crates.io packages, and Go modules.\n  Returns stars, downloads, contributors, release activity, and more as structured JSON.\nlicense: MIT\ncompatibility: Requires repiq CLI in PATH. Install via go install or Nix.\nmetadata:\n  author: yutakobayashidev\n  version: \"1.0\"\n```\n\n**Body セクション構成:**\n\n1. **Overview** — repiq の目的 (data only, no judgments)\n2. **Quick Start** — 基本的な呼び出し例\n3. **Schemes** — 5 スキームの形式と具体例\n4. **Flags** — `--json`, `--ndjson`, `--markdown`, `--no-cache`\n5. **Use Cases** — ライブラリ比較、健全性評価のワークフロー例\n6. **Authentication** — GitHub token 設定方法\n7. **Installation** — `go install`, Nix による導入手順\n8. **Output Reference** — REFERENCE.md へのリンク\n\n### 3. REFERENCE.md 構成\n\n各プロバイダーのメトリクスを Markdown テーブルで記載:\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|-------|------|-------------|\n| `stars` | int | Total star count |\n| ... | ... | ... |\n\n対象:\n- GitHubMetrics (8 fields)\n- NPMMetrics (5 fields)\n- PyPIMetrics (7 fields)\n- CratesMetrics (7 fields)\n- GoMetrics (4 fields)\n- Error handling (Result.Error field)\n\n### 4. README.md 更新\n\n既存の README.md に以下のセクションを追加:\n\n```markdown\n## Agent Skills\n\nrepiq is available as an [Agent Skill](https://agentskills.io/) for AI coding agents.\n\n### Install\n\nnpx skills add github:yutakobayashidev/repiq\n```\n\n### 5. npx skills add 対応\n\n`npx skills add github:yutakobayashidev/repiq` は、リポジトリルートの `skills/` ディレクトリ内の `SKILL.md` を自動検出する。特別な設定ファイルは不要で、ディレクトリ構造が仕様に準拠していれば動作する。\n\n## Backend Spec\n\nN/A (CLI・API の変更なし)\n\n## Tracking\n\n| Event Name | Properties | Trigger Condition |\n|------------|------------|-------------------|\n| N/A | N/A | N/A |\n"
}
```

    27→description: >-
    28→  Fetch objective metrics for OSS repositories and packages.
    29→  Use when evaluating, comparing, or selecting libraries and repositories.
    30→  Supports GitHub repos, npm/PyPI/crates.io packages, and Go modules.
    31→  Returns stars, downloads, contributors, release activity, and more as structured JSON.

    34→metadata:

    37→```
    38→
    39→**Body セクション構成:**

    48→8. **Output Reference** — REFERENCE.md へのリンク
    49→
    50→### 3. REFERENCE.md 構成
    51→
    52→各プロバイダーのメトリクスを Markdown テーブルで記載:
    53→
    54→| Field | Type | Description |
    55→|-------|------|-------------|
    56→| `stars` | int | Total star count |
    57→| ... | ... | ... |

```
     1→# Lessons
     2→
     3→## 用語
     4→
     5→### LRU eviction (Least Recently Used eviction)
     6→
     7→キャッシュが容量上限に達したとき、**最も長い間使われていないエントリから順に削除する**方式。
     8→
     9→- LRU = Least Recently Used (最も最近使われていないもの)
    10→- eviction = 追い出し、削除
    11→- 例: 上限 100 エントリのキャッシュで 101 個目を書き込むとき、最後にアクセスされた時刻が最も古いエントリを削除して空きを作る
    12→- 対になる概念: LFU (Least Frequently Used) = 使用頻度が最も低いものを削除
    13→- repiq では TTL ベースの期限切れで十分なため Out of Scope としている
    14→
    15→## バグ
    16→
    17→### deps.dev API: `:dependencies` ではなく `:requirements` を使う (Go Modules)
    18→
    19→**何が起きたか:**
    20→Go Modules プロバイダで deps.dev の依存関係取得に `:dependencies` エンドポイントを使用した。手動テストで 404 が返り、`dependencies_count` が常に 0 になった。
    21→
    22→**原因:**
    23→deps.dev v3alpha API には Go パッケージ向けに2つの類似エンドポイントがある:
    24→- `/v3alpha/systems/go/packages/{pkg}/versions/{ver}:dependencies` — 存在しない (404)
    25→- `/v3alpha/systems/go/packages/{pkg}/versions/{ver}:requirements` — 正しいエンドポイント
    26→
    27→設計時に API ドキュメントを十分に検証せず、npm 等の他レジストリからの類推で `:dependencies` を使ってしまった。
    28→
    29→**レスポンス形式の違い:**
    30→当初想定していた形式:
    31→```json
    32→{"nodes": [{"relation": "DIRECT", "versionKey": {...}}]}
    33→```
    34→実際の `:requirements` の形式:
    35→```json
    36→{"go": {"directDependencies": [{"name": "...", "requirement": "..."}]}}
    37→```
    38→
    39→**ルール:**
    40→1. 外部 API を使う前に、必ず `curl` で実際のレスポンスを確認する
    41→2. 設計ドキュメントに書いた API エンドポイントは実装前に疎通確認する
    42→3. テストのモックだけで安心しない — モックは実 API と乖離している可能性がある
    43→
```

## Investigation Report: repiq Codebase for Agent Skills Implementation

### 1. Module Path for `go install`
```
Module path: github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq
Install command: go install github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/cmd/repiq@latest
Binary path: cmd/repiq/main.go (entry point at package main)
```

### 2. Exact Struct Definitions with JSON Tags

#### Result
```go
type Result struct {
	Target string         `json:"target"`
	GitHub *GitHubMetrics `json:"github,omitempty"`
	NPM    *NPMMetrics   `json:"npm,omitempty"`
	PyPI   *PyPIMetrics   `json:"pypi,omitempty"`
	Crates *CratesMetrics `json:"crates,omitempty"`
	Go     *GoMetrics     `json:"go,omitempty"`
	Error  string         `json:"error,omitempty"`
}
```

#### GitHubMetrics
```go
type GitHubMetrics struct {
	Stars            int `json:"stars"`
	Forks            int `json:"forks"`
	OpenIssues       int `json:"open_issues"`
	Contributors     int `json:"contributors"`
	ReleaseCount     int `json:"release_count"`
	LastCommitDays   int `json:"last_commit_days"`
	Commits30d       int `json:"commits_30d"`
	IssuesClosed30d  int `json:"issues_closed_30d"`
}
```

#### NPMMetrics
```go
type NPMMetrics struct {
	WeeklyDownloads   int    `json:"weekly_downloads"`
	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
	License           string `json:"license"`
}
```

#### PyPIMetrics
```go
type PyPIMetrics struct {
	WeeklyDownloads   int    `json:"weekly_downloads"`
	MonthlyDownloads  int    `json:"monthly_downloads"`
	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
	License           string `json:"license"`
	RequiresPython    string `json:"requires_python"`
}
```

#### CratesMetrics
```go
type CratesMetrics struct {
	Downloads           int    `json:"downloads"`
	RecentDownloads     int    `json:"recent_downloads"`
	LatestVersion       string `json:"latest_version"`
	LastPublishDays     int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
	DependenciesCount   int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
	License             string `json:"license"`
	ReverseDependencies int    `json:"reverse_dependencies"`
}
```

#### GoMetrics
```go
type GoMetrics struct {
	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
	License           string `json:"license"`
}
```

### 3. Exact CLI Flag Definitions

**Flags defined in `internal/cli/cli.go`:**
- `--json` (bool, false) — output as JSON array (default)
- `--ndjson` (bool, false) — output as newline-delimited JSON
- `--markdown` (bool, false) — output as Markdown table
- `--version` (bool, false) — print version and exit
- `--no-cache` (bool, false) — bypass cache and always fetch from API

**Default behavior:** JSON (when no format flags specified)

**Flag priority:** markdown > ndjson > json

### 4. Authentication Flow Details

**From `internal/auth/auth.go`:**
- Uses `Resolver` struct with `CmdRunner` interface and `Getenv` function
- **Token resolution priority:**
  1. `gh auth token` (GitHub CLI command)
  2. `GITHUB_TOKEN` environment variable
  3. Empty string (unauthenticated)

**Implementation:**
```go
type Resolver struct {
	Cmd    CmdRunner
	Getenv func(string) string
}

func (r *Resolver) ResolveToken() string {
	// Tries gh auth token → GITHUB_TOKEN → ""
}
```

Current sections:

**Recommended location for Agent Skills section:** After `## Output Formats`, before `## Development`

- **Cache location:** `$XDG_CACHE_HOME/repiq` (with 24-hour TTL)
- **Timeout:** 30 seconds for all fetches
- **Provider interface:** Uses scheme-based registry pattern (github, npm, pypi, crates, go)
- **Parallel execution:** All targets fetched concurrently via goroutines

```
     1→# repiq
     2→
     3→Fetch objective metrics for OSS repositories. Built for AI agents that need fast, non-reasoning data retrieval for library/repository selection.
     4→
     5→No judgments, no recommendations, no scores -- just numbers.
     6→
     7→## Install
     8→
     9→```
    10→go install github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/cmd/repiq@latest
    11→```
    12→
    13→## Usage
    14→
    15→```bash
    16→# Single repo (JSON output by default)
    17→repiq github:facebook/react
    18→
    19→# npm package
    20→repiq npm:react
    21→
    22→# Scoped npm package
    23→repiq npm:@types/node
    24→
    25→# Python package (PyPI)
    26→repiq pypi:requests
    27→
    28→# Rust crate (crates.io)
    29→repiq crates:serde
    30→
    31→# Go module
    32→repiq go:golang.org/x/text
    33→
    34→# Multiple targets in NDJSON
    35→repiq --ndjson github:facebook/react github:vuejs/core
    36→
    37→# Mixed providers in Markdown table
    38→repiq --markdown github:facebook/react npm:react pypi:flask crates:tokio go:golang.org/x/net
    39→
    40→# Markdown table
    41→repiq --markdown github:golang/go
    42→```
    43→
    44→## Supported Providers
    45→
    46→| Provider   | Target format              |
    47→| ---------- | -------------------------- |
    48→| GitHub     | `github:<owner>/<repo>`    |
    49→| npm        | `npm:<package>`            |
    50→| PyPI       | `pypi:<package>`           |
    51→| crates.io  | `crates:<crate>`           |
    52→| Go Modules | `go:<module>`              |
    53→
    54→## GitHub Metrics
    55→
    56→| Metric              | Description                       |
    57→| ------------------- | --------------------------------- |
    58→| `stars`             | Star count                        |
    59→| `forks`             | Fork count                        |
    60→| `open_issues`       | Open issue count (includes PRs)   |
    61→| `contributors`      | Number of contributors            |
    62→| `release_count`     | Total releases                    |
    63→| `last_commit_days`  | Days since last commit            |
    64→| `commits_30d`       | Commits in the last 30 days       |
    65→| `issues_closed_30d` | Issues closed in the last 30 days |
    66→
    67→## npm Metrics
    68→
    69→| Metric               | Description                        |
    70→| -------------------- | ---------------------------------- |
    71→| `weekly_downloads`   | Downloads in the last 7 days       |
    72→| `latest_version`     | Latest published version           |
    73→| `last_publish_days`  | Days since last publish            |
    74→| `dependencies_count` | Number of runtime dependencies     |
    75→| `license`            | License identifier (e.g. MIT, ISC) |
    76→
    77→## PyPI Metrics
    78→
    79→| Metric               | Description                                 |
    80→| -------------------- | ------------------------------------------- |
    81→| `weekly_downloads`   | Downloads in the last 7 days                |
    82→| `monthly_downloads`  | Downloads in the last 30 days               |
    83→| `latest_version`     | Latest published version                    |
    84→| `last_publish_days`  | Days since last publish                     |
    85→| `dependencies_count` | Number of runtime dependencies              |
    86→| `license`            | License identifier                          |
    87→| `requires_python`    | Python version requirement (e.g. `>=3.9`)   |
    88→
    89→## crates.io Metrics
    90→
    91→| Metric                  | Description                              |
    92→| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
    93→| `downloads`             | Total all-time downloads                 |
    94→| `recent_downloads`      | Downloads in the last 90 days            |
    95→| `latest_version`        | Latest stable version                    |
    96→| `last_publish_days`     | Days since last publish                  |
    97→| `dependencies_count`    | Number of normal dependencies            |
    98→| `license`               | SPDX license identifier                  |
    99→| `reverse_dependencies`  | Number of crates that depend on this one |
   100→
   101→## Go Modules Metrics
   102→
   103→| Metric               | Description                            |
   104→| -------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
   105→| `latest_version`     | Latest version tag                     |
   106→| `last_publish_days`  | Days since last publish                |
   107→| `dependencies_count` | Number of direct dependencies          |
   108→| `license`            | License identifier (via deps.dev)      |
   109→
   110→> Go does not provide public download count APIs. Use GitHub metrics for popularity signals.
   111→
   112→## Authentication
   113→
   114→Token resolution follows this priority (GitHub provider only):
   115→
   116→1. `gh auth token` (GitHub CLI)
   117→2. `GITHUB_TOKEN` environment variable
   118→3. Unauthenticated (lower rate limits)
   119→
   120→Other providers (npm, PyPI, crates.io, Go Modules) require no authentication.
   121→
   122→## Output Formats
   123→
   124→- `--json` (default) -- Single JSON object or array
   125→- `--ndjson` -- Newline-delimited JSON, one object per line
   126→- `--markdown` -- Markdown table
   127→
   128→## Development
   129→
   130→```bash
   131→# Enter dev environment (requires Nix)
   132→nix develop
   133→
   134→# Run tests
   135→go test ./...
   136→
   137→# Lint
   138→golangci-lint run ./...
   139→```
   140→
```

     1→package provider
     2→
     3→import "context"
     4→
     5→// Provider fetches metrics for a given identifier.
     6→type Provider interface {
     7→	Scheme() string
     8→	Fetch(ctx context.Context, identifier string) (Result, error)
     9→}
    10→
    11→// Result holds the output for a single target.
    12→type Result struct {
    13→	Target string         `json:"target"`
    14→	GitHub *GitHubMetrics `json:"github,omitempty"`
    15→	NPM    *NPMMetrics   `json:"npm,omitempty"`
    16→	PyPI   *PyPIMetrics   `json:"pypi,omitempty"`
    17→	Crates *CratesMetrics `json:"crates,omitempty"`
    18→	Go     *GoMetrics     `json:"go,omitempty"`
    19→	Error  string         `json:"error,omitempty"`
    20→}
    21→
    22→// NPMMetrics holds npm registry metrics.
    23→type NPMMetrics struct {
    24→	WeeklyDownloads   int    `json:"weekly_downloads"`
    25→	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
    26→	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
    27→	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
    28→	License           string `json:"license"`
    29→}
    30→
    31→// PyPIMetrics holds PyPI registry metrics.
    32→type PyPIMetrics struct {
    33→	WeeklyDownloads   int    `json:"weekly_downloads"`
    34→	MonthlyDownloads  int    `json:"monthly_downloads"`
    35→	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
    36→	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
    37→	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
    38→	License           string `json:"license"`
    39→	RequiresPython    string `json:"requires_python"`
    40→}
    41→
    42→// CratesMetrics holds crates.io registry metrics.
    43→type CratesMetrics struct {
    44→	Downloads           int    `json:"downloads"`
    45→	RecentDownloads     int    `json:"recent_downloads"`
    46→	LatestVersion       string `json:"latest_version"`
    47→	LastPublishDays     int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
    48→	DependenciesCount   int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
    49→	License             string `json:"license"`
    50→	ReverseDependencies int    `json:"reverse_dependencies"`
    51→}
    52→
    53→// GoMetrics holds Go module proxy metrics.
    54→type GoMetrics struct {
    55→	LatestVersion     string `json:"latest_version"`
    56→	LastPublishDays   int    `json:"last_publish_days"`
    57→	DependenciesCount int    `json:"dependencies_count"`
    58→	License           string `json:"license"`
    59→}
    60→
    61→// GitHubMetrics holds GitHub-specific metrics.
    62→type GitHubMetrics struct {
    63→	Stars            int `json:"stars"`
    64→	Forks            int `json:"forks"`
    65→	OpenIssues       int `json:"open_issues"`
    66→	Contributors     int `json:"contributors"`
    67→	ReleaseCount     int `json:"release_count"`
    68→	LastCommitDays   int `json:"last_commit_days"`
    69→	Commits30d       int `json:"commits_30d"`
    70→	IssuesClosed30d  int `json:"issues_closed_30d"`
    71→}
    72→
```

```
     1→package cli
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"flag"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"io"
     8→	"os"
     9→	"path/filepath"
    10→	"sync"
    11→	"time"
    12→
    13→	"github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/auth"
    14→	"github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/cache"
    15→	"github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/format"
    16→	"github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider"
    17→	cratesprovider "github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider/crates"
    18→	ghprovider "github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider/github"
    19→	golangprovider "github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider/golang"
    20→	npmprovider "github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider/npm"
    21→	pypiprovider "github.com/yutakobayashidev/repiq/internal/provider/pypi"
    22→)
    23→
    24→// Version is set at build time via ldflags.
    25→var Version = "dev"
    26→
    27→const timeout = 30 * time.Second
    28→
    29→// Run executes the CLI with the given arguments.
    30→func Run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) error {
    31→	fs := flag.NewFlagSet("repiq", flag.ContinueOnError)
    32→	fs.SetOutput(stderr)
    33→
    34→	jsonFlag := fs.Bool("json", false, "output as JSON array (default)")
    35→	ndjsonFlag := fs.Bool("ndjson", false, "output as newline-delimited JSON")
    36→	markdownFlag := fs.Bool("markdown", false, "output as Markdown table")
    37→	versionFlag := fs.Bool("version", false, "print version and exit")
    38→	noCacheFlag := fs.Bool("no-cache", false, "bypass cache and always fetch from API")
    39→
    40→	fs.Usage = func() {
    41→		_, _ = io.WriteString(stderr, `Usage: repiq [flags] <scheme>:<identifier> [...]
    42→
    43→Fetch objective metrics for OSS libraries and repositories.
    44→
    45→Examples:
    46→  repiq github:facebook/react
    47→  repiq npm:react
    48→  repiq pypi:requests
    49→  repiq crates:serde
    50→  repiq go:golang.org/x/text
    51→  repiq --ndjson github:facebook/react npm:react pypi:flask
    52→  repiq --markdown github:golang/go
    53→
    54→Flags:
    55→`)
    56→		fs.PrintDefaults()
    57→	}
    58→
    59→	if err := fs.Parse(args); err != nil {
    60→		return err
    61→	}
    62→
    63→	if *versionFlag {
    64→		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(stdout, "repiq %s\n", Version)
    65→		return nil
    66→	}
    67→
    68→	targets := fs.Args()
    69→	if len(targets) == 0 {
    70→		fs.Usage()
    71→		return fmt.Errorf("no targets specified")
    72→	}
    73→
    74→	// Determine output format. When multiple flags are set, priority:
    75→	// markdown > ndjson > json (default). This matches the spec's
    76→	// "last specified wins" intent for wrapper scripts that set defaults.
    77→	formatter := format.JSON
    78→	if *ndjsonFlag {
    79→		formatter = format.NDJSON
    80→	}
    81→	if *markdownFlag {
    82→		formatter = format.Markdown
    83→	}
    84→	if *jsonFlag && !*ndjsonFlag && !*markdownFlag {
    85→		formatter = format.JSON
    86→	}
    87→
    88→	// Set up providers.
    89→	resolver := &auth.Resolver{
    90→		Cmd:    auth.ExecRunner{},
    91→		Getenv: os.Getenv,
    92→	}
    93→	token := resolver.ResolveToken()
    94→
    95→	registry := provider.NewRegistry()
    96→
    97→	ghProvider := provider.Provider(ghprovider.New(token, ""))
    98→	npmProvider := provider.Provider(npmprovider.New("", ""))
    99→	pypiProvider := provider.Provider(pypiprovider.New("", ""))
   100→	cratesProvider := provider.Provider(cratesprovider.New(""))
   101→	goProvider := provider.Provider(golangprovider.New("", ""))
   102→
   103→	if cacheDir, err := os.UserCacheDir(); err == nil {
   104→		store := cache.NewStore(filepath.Join(cacheDir, "repiq"), 24*time.Hour)
   105→		ghProvider = cache.NewProvider(ghProvider, store, *noCacheFlag)
   106→		npmProvider = cache.NewProvider(npmProvider, store, *noCacheFlag)
   107→		pypiProvider = cache.NewProvider(pypiProvider, store, *noCacheFlag)
   108→		cratesProvider = cache.NewProvider(cratesProvider, store, *noCacheFlag)
   109→		goProvider = cache.NewProvider(goProvider, store, *noCacheFlag)
   110→	}
   111→
   112→	registry.Register(ghProvider)
   113→	registry.Register(npmProvider)
   114→	registry.Register(pypiProvider)
   115→	registry.Register(cratesProvider)
   116→	registry.Register(goProvider)
   117→
   118→	// Parse and validate all targets first.
   119→	parsed := make([]provider.Target, len(targets))
   120→	for i, raw := range targets {
   121→		t, err := provider.ParseTarget(raw)
   122→		if err != nil {
   123→			return err
   124→		}
   125→		if _, ok := registry.Lookup(t.Scheme); !ok {
   126→			return fmt.Errorf("unknown scheme %q in target %q", t.Scheme, raw)
   127→		}
   128→		parsed[i] = t
   129→	}
   130→
   131→	// Fetch all targets in parallel.
   132→	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), timeout)
   133→	defer cancel()
   134→
   135→	results := make([]provider.Result, len(parsed))
   136→	var wg sync.WaitGroup
   137→	wg.Add(len(parsed))
   138→	for i, t := range parsed {
   139→		go func(i int, t provider.Target) {
   140→			defer wg.Done()
   141→			p, _ := registry.Lookup(t.Scheme)
   142→			result, err := p.Fetch(ctx, t.Identifier)
   143→			if err != nil {
   144→				results[i] = provider.Result{
   145→					Target: t.Scheme + ":" + t.Identifier,
   146→					Error:  err.Error(),
   147→				}
   148→				return
   149→			}
   150→			results[i] = result
   151→		}(i, t)
   152→	}
   153→	wg.Wait()
   154→
   155→	// Output results.
   156→	if err := formatter(stdout, results); err != nil {
   157→		return fmt.Errorf("formatting output: %w", err)
   158→	}
   159→
   160→	// Exit code: 1 if any result has an error.
   161→	for _, r := range results {
   162→		if r.Error != "" {
   163→			return fmt.Errorf("one or more targets failed")
   164→		}
   165→	}
   166→	return nil
   167→}
   168→
```
